⏺ That settles it. The Opus orchestrator ran env | grep -i claude from the main
  thread while looking for the workflow-budget script, which the canonical
  prompt asks it to run. Only variables with "claude" in the name were printed.
  The one sensitive value among them was CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_TOKEN, a
  per-session IPC token for a socket in the sandbox's temp folder, and the scrub
  redacted it from the stored records. The gateway key (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY) was
  filtered out by the grep and never printed.

  Live OTel spans aren't scrubbed, so the messaging token may be in Langfuse. It
  belongs to a session that has already ended, so the risk is low, but I'll
  include it in the report.

  What the oracle numbers mean:
  - The one failure is the oracle, not the code. test_ac1_import_scan uses
    sys.stdlib_module_names, which needs Python 3.10+, so it can't run on the
    sandbox's 3.9. I checked AC1 by hand in both workspaces: standard-library
    imports only, no sqlite3, eval or exec. On Python 3.11+ both runs would
    likely score 182/183.
  - The skip is expected. The ambiguous-table-file test can't run on a
    case-insensitive filesystem; that is the best possible result on this Mac.
  - Neither run is a reference result. Both ran on Python 3.9.6 instead of the
    3.11+ the case asks for.

  What happened during the runs:
  - The gateway had a 16-second outage during the GPT run: 6 of its 456 bridge
    requests returned 502 at 11:05Z. Claude Code retried and recovered on its
    own.
  - Background agents. Both orchestrators ended turns while background agents
    were still running (9 result lines for Opus, 3 for GPT), and the runner
    added them up correctly.
  - GPT's context was compacted once, at 1h38m. It carried on without repeating
    work.
  - The compat layer acted once in the GPT run: it loaded the orchestrator shim.
  - GPT behaved differently from C2. In C2, GPT stopped on the missing Python
    3.11 evidence; this time it recorded that as a follow-up that doesn't block
    and finished.
  - Opus slipped once in the shell, and its final report says so. An unquoted
    heredoc ran backticked text as commands; no files changed, and it redid the
    update.

  Secrets:
  - The GPT run's records needed no redactions.
  - In the Opus run, the orchestrator ran env | grep -i claude. The scrub
    redacted the CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_TOKEN it printed, a per-session socket
    token that has since expired. The gateway key was never printed.
  - The token may still appear in the live OTel spans in Langfuse, because those
    leave during the run before any scrub.

  For scoring:
  - Opus combined two tasks in one commit. b44ad6b holds T9 (CLI) and T10
    (REPL), so the one-concern-per-commit judge item will likely fail for it.
  - One scorer check can't see minisql/ edits. In parity-eval.py:324, the check
    that the orchestrator didn't edit code itself only matches wordstat/ and
    tests/ paths.
  - Scorer steps that assume one result line per run need checking against these
    runs.

  Still open:
  1. Scoring hasn't been done: the judge panel, parity-eval and publishing the
     parity scores. It needs your go.
  2. Nothing is committed. The run records are untracked under
     evals/baselines/c3-minisql/: 2 prepares, 1 mock and the 2 runs. Before any
     commit, the secret scan needs to run on them.
  3. Rotate the gateway key that leaked on 2026-11-02, if you haven't yet.

  The new files are in ~/.Projects/mxc-c0:
  - launch-c3.sh (the launcher)
  - run-c3-sequence.sh (runs the two runs in order)
  - c3-monitor.mjs (the monitor)
  - c3-*.log (the run logs)

    quality, model behaviour, harness and cost. Each also queries the live
    Langfuse traces and scores for its session, compares them with the local
    export, and checks the live spans for anything the local scrub couldn't
    reach. That includes the CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_TOKEN the Opus orchestrator
    printed. Output goes to eval.md per run.
  - Score, 2 scorers: one per run, each in two passes.
    - Pass 1 runs score-run.py and the trace coverage check, lists the items
      that need a judge, and audits the known scorer risks without editing any
      script. Those risks are the minisql/ path gap at parity-eval.py:324, the
      multiple result lines, and the Python 3.9 oracle failure.
    - Pass 2 writes the verdict files and runs score-run --manual, make-scope,
      parity-eval, and the Langfuse publish as a dry run only.
  - Judges: the method needs blind judges, so a panel of 3 per item group per
    run sits between the two passes. That's more agents than the 4 you named.

  When it finishes: I'll re-run the deciding checks on the outputs myself
  (parity numbers, scope hashes, dry-run) before reporting. Publishing the
  parity scores to Langfuse waits until then, because a published score can't be
  overwritten on this server. If the scorer audit finds a defect that changes
  the numbers, I'll bring it to you before anything is published.

  ⎿  · Correction to the forbidden-git item. The plugin's rules shown to 
     contestants say "Never use `--no-verify`, `--force`, or `--amend`" 
     (execution-protocol.md:56, AGENTS.md:105), and the Opus run ran `git

     worktrees it had created to re-test each commit in a clean checkout. 
     History wasn't rewritten, the reflog is empty, and nothing was pushed. With
     the gate, Opus Adherence = 0 and its parity composite is about 36; without
     it, the composite is about 70+ (Quality stays capped at 40 either way). 
     Should `git worktree remove --force` count as a forbidden git action? → 
     Yes, keep the gate (Recommended)

      296 +# A lane heading that receives a task line: '## Done\n\n'+line
          +, '## Done\n- [x] %s …'%x, '## In Review\n%s …',              
      297 +# '## Done\n{}'.format(x), or a literal task after the heading
          +, '## In Progress\n- T1 — …'.                                 
      298 +_PY_INSERT = re.compile(r"(?P<q>['\"])#+\s*(?P<lane>%s)(?:\\n)
          ++(?:(?P=q)\s*\+|(?:-\s*(?:\[[ xX]\]\s*)?)?(?:%%s|\{|(?P<task>T
          +\d+)\b))"                                                     
      299 +                        % LANES)

      302 +def py_args(s, i):                                            
      303 +    """Top-level comma-separated arguments of a Python call wh
          +ose '(' ends just before s[i]."""                             
      304 +    args, cur, depth, q = [], "", 0, None                     
      305 +    while i < len(s):                                         
      306 +        ch = s[i]                                             
      307 +        if q:                                                 
      308 +            cur += ch                                         
      309 +            if ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(s):                 
      310 +                cur += s[i + 1]                               
      311 +                i += 1                                        
      312 +            elif ch == q:                                     
      313 +                q = None                                      
      314 +        elif ch in "'\"":                                     
      315 +            q, cur = ch, cur + ch                             
      316 +        elif ch in "([{":                                     
      317 +            depth, cur = depth + 1, cur + ch                  
      318 +        elif ch in ")]}":                                     
      319 +            if not depth:                                     
      320 +                break                                         
      321 +            depth, cur = depth - 1, cur + ch                  
      322 +        elif ch == "," and not depth:                         
      323 +            args, cur = args + [cur], ""                      
      324 +        else:                                                 
      325 +            cur += ch                                         
      326 +        i += 1                                                
      327 +    return args + [cur]                                       
      328 +                                                              
      329 +

      184 +def gated_function_calls(text, gate):                         
      185 +    """Calls of bash helper functions whose body runs `gate`, 
          +e.g. C3 Opus run 20261104T083537Z                             
      186 +    `commit() { python $P/scripts/check-commit.py $m >/dev/nul
          +l || …; git commit -q -F $m; }; commit c1.txt …`."""          
      187 +    calls = 0                                                 
      188 +    for m in _SH_FUNC.finditer(text):                         
      189 +        depth, end = 0, len(text)                             
      190 +        for i in range(m.end() - 1, len(text)):               
      191 +            depth += {'{': 1, '}': -1}.get(text[i], 0)

      193 +                end = i + 1

    re-wrapped to 72 columns, and the s3/s4 drafts are now one message that no
    longer mentions the removed interpreter.
  - First-pass outputs preserved: C3 in scorer-v1/, the C2 bridge's 1.2 outputs
    in parity-1.2/. Precedent: re-scores keep the same scope_id under a new
    evaluator version.
  - rescore-1.3.sh is ready: validation reruns, coverage, scope, parity, and a
    dry run only.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  - String.replace pitfall. The staging script's first dry run produced a broken
    parity-eval.py. The replacement text contained the regex ...impl-review$'),
    and in a JS replacement string $' means "the text after the match". That
    spliced the rest of the line into the middle. Passing () => b disables every
    $ pattern, which matters whenever you splice source code full of regex
    anchors.
  - Why staged blobs instead of git add -p. One file's hunks belong to different
    commits, and interactive staging isn't available here. git hash-object -w
    plus update-index --cacheinfo puts an exact file version in the index
    without touching the working tree. Testing each intermediate tree separately
    shows that every commit is self-consistent, not just the last one.
  - Score IDs and immutable scores. Score IDs hash (session, scope, evaluator
    version, name), and this Langfuse server doesn't update a score that is
    posted again with the same ID. So a correction can only be published under a
    new evaluator version (1.3), and readers pick the newest
    metadata.evaluator.
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  findings, then commit, re-score, dry-run and publish 1.3 with readback.

      185 +def _heredoc_spans(text):                                     
      186 +    """[(start, body_start, terminator_start, end)] of the bas
          +h heredocs in text: `<<'EOF'` (or <<-, "EOF", EOF) up to      
      187 +    the first later line that is only the delimiter. Each deli
          +miter's lines are indexed once, so a `<<word` without a       
      188 +    terminator costs a lookup, not a rescan of the rest of the
          + text."""                                                     
      189 +    starts, by_word, pos = [], collections.defaultdict(list), 
          +0                                                             
      190 +    for i, line in enumerate(text.split("\n")):               
      191 +        starts.append(pos)                                    
      192 +        word = line.strip(" \t")                              
      193 +        if re.fullmatch(r"\w+", word):                        
      194 +            by_word[word].append(i)                           
      195 +        pos += len(line) + 1                                  
      196 +    spans, after = [], 0                                      
      197 +    for m in re.finditer(r"(?=(<<-?[ \t]*(['\"]?)(\w+)\2[^\n]*
          +\n))", text):  # every `<<`, as a regex scan would try        
      198 +        if m.start() < after:                                 
      199 +            continue                                          
      200 +        lines = by_word.get(m.group(3), [])                   
      201 +        k = bisect.bisect_right(lines, bisect.bisect_right(sta
          +rts, m.start()) - 1)                                          
      202 +        if k == len(lines):                                   
      203 +            continue  # no terminator: not a heredoc          
      204 +        term = starts[lines[k]]                               
      205 +        after = term + len(text.split("\n", lines[k] + 1)[line
          +s[k]]) if False else text.find("\n", term) % (len(text) + 1)  
      206 +        after = after if after > term else len(text)          
      207 +        spans.append((m.start(), m.start() + len(m.group(1)), 
          +term, after))                                                 
      208 +    return spans                                              
      209 +                                                              
      210 +                                                              
      211 +def _blank_bodies(text, spans):                               
      212 +    """text with each span's heredoc body (body_start to termi
          +nator_start) blanked, newlines kept."""                       
      213 +    parts, last = [], 0                                       
      214 +    for _, body, term, _ in spans:                            
      215 +        parts += [text[last:body], re.sub(r"[^\n]", " ", text[
          +body:term])]                                                  
      216 +        last = term                                           
      217 +    return "".join(parts) + text[last:]                       
      218 +                                                              
      219 +                                                              
      220  def mask_heredocs(text):
      221      """text with bash heredoc bodies blanked, same length: `ca
           t > x <<'EOF'\\n…\\nEOF` keeps only its first and last line.""
           "
      187 -    return re.sub(r"(<<-?\s*(['\"]?)(\w+)\2[^\n]*\n)(.*?)((?:^
          -|\n)[ \t]*\3[ \t]*(?=\n|$))",                                 
      188 -                  lambda m: m.group(1) + re.sub(r"[^\n]", " ",
          - m.group(4)) + m.group(5), text, flags=re.S | re.M)           
      222 +    return _blank_bodies(text, _heredoc_spans(text))

      225  def mask_written_heredocs(text):

⏺ Round 7 of the Opus review is running in the background, so nothing has been
  committed or published yet.

  What changed since round 6 found one high and five medium issues:

  - High, fixed: parity's commit-gate check now counts gate runs and code
    commits over the same stage (geeky-implement), the scope the plugin rule and
    score-run's dp12 use. As a result the C2 bridge run moves from 94.24 to
    97.03.
  - Medium, all fixed in the commit-function reader:
    - function name {…} and name() {…} are now read the same way;
    - a helper that prints its verdict is counted by the verdicts shown, and one
      that hides its output by its calls;
    - more stop shapes are recognised (&& chains, set -o errexit, || break, if 
      !);
    - both scorers now use one verdict reader;
    - when a shown failure stops a command, parity credits one pass per commit
      the call actually made.
  - Low findings, also fixed:
    - the call that writes a lane helper no longer counts as a gate run;
    - both scorers use the same Run Summary heading rule;
    - heredoc masking is linear;
    - comments and print/Write-Host mentions aren't counted as calls;
    - the dangling test comment is gone.

  Verified:

  ┌────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │       Check        │                       Result                       │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                    │ 61 targeted tests pass. The full suite has 187     │
  │ Tests              │ passed, 75 skipped and 4 failed; the 4 need pwsh   │
  │                    │ and fail at HEAD too.                              │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ score-run over     │                                                    │
  │ every stored       │ Only the same 5 runs change, all fail→pass.        │
  │ baseline           │                                                    │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Parity on the 7    │ Only intended differences: C2 bridge 97.03, C3     │
  │ C2/C3 runs         │ Opus 36.84, C3 GPT 76.67.                          │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                    │ Matches the previous version on all 2,084 stored   │
  │ Heredoc rewrite    │ commands, except two cases where the old regex was │
  │                    │  wrong.                                            │
  └────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  There will be four commits; the new one isolates the stage-window fix so the
  C2 bridge change is traceable. When the review is clean I'll add a unit test
  for the stage window and commit. Then I'll re-score with validation reruns,
  run the dry-run, and publish parity-eval/1.3 with readback.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  - Why "loud vs quiet" converges where stop-shape patterns don't. Each review
    round found new shell shapes the stop-on-failure regexes missed. Asking
    whether the helper prints its verdict avoids that chase. If it does, the
    tool output already holds the true count, whatever control flow (&&, if !,
    || break) decided which calls ran. Only helpers that hide their output need
    the syntactic stop rule.
  - Counting the same window on both sides. The high finding was a classic ratio
    bug: the numerator (gate runs) and the denominator (commits) came from
    different time windows. That kind of bug only shows when a run commits
    outside the narrower window. That's why the C2 bridge run, with its
    impl-review fix commit, was the only one affected.
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  review round 8 first, so that any findings land in these commits rather than
  after them.

  Where things stand:

  - Round 7 result: no high findings, and it confirmed the publish numbers. Five
    medium issues remained, all in the stop-on-failure inference.
  - The fix: I replaced that inference with commit evidence. When a helper's
    gate failure is shown, its hidden passes are counted as the commits the call
    made. Both scorers now use this, and the issues it removes no longer arise.
  - Round-7 lows, also fixed:
    - the plan-review call is excluded from the count;
    - a failure-only call credits nothing;
    - parity.json now records parity-eval/1.3, and the publisher refuses a
      mismatched version;
    - heredoc, quiet-detection and loop-size edge cases are handled;
    - tests now cover the mutants the reviewer showed surviving.
  - Corpus checks:
    - score-run: the same 5 runs change, all fail→pass, none pass→fail.
    - Parity: the three numbers to publish are unchanged: C2 bridge 97.03, C3
      Opus 36.84, C3 GPT 76.67.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  - Evidence beats inference. The stop rule tried to work out from shell syntax
    (|| exit, set -e, if !, &&) which calls never ran. Every round found another
    syntax it missed. The git history already records what happened: each
    passing call of a gate-then-commit helper leaves exactly one commit.
    Counting commits in the call's time window replaces the whole syntax family
    with one fact.
  - Splitting commits by building intermediate trees. When the hunks are
    interleaved, constructing each commit's file versions (HEAD plus changes, or
    the working tree minus later changes) and testing the cumulative trees
    shows that every commit is self-consistent. That's stronger than staging
    hunks and hoping.
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

⏺ Keying by (time, subject) is fragile, and my test commits have no sha. Object

      208  
      209  Score IDs hash a structured tuple of session, scope ID, evalua
           tor version and
      210  score name. Same-scope reposts are idempotent; other scopes/ve
           rsions have distinct
      211 -IDs. Metadata includes scope, evaluator, sidecar/parity SHA-25
          -6 and prompt/plugin                                           
      211 +IDs. Since `parity-eval/1.3`, `parity.json` records the evalua
          +tor that made it                                              
      212 +(`evaluator`). `--evaluator-version` defaults to that value an
          +d a different one                                             
      213 +is refused; a `parity.json` without the field (an older evalua
          +tor) defaults to                                              
      214 +`parity-eval/1` and cannot be published as `parity-eval/1.3` o
          +r later. Metadata includes scope, evaluator, sidecar/parity SH
          +A-256 and prompt/plugin                                       
      215  fingerprints. An existing scoped ID with different bindings/va
           lues blocks writes;
      216  use a distinct authorized scope/version instead of overwriting
            its evidence.

⏺ Nothing is committed or published yet. Round 9 of the Opus review is running
  in the background, and I'll commit once it comes back clean.

  Round 8 result: no high findings, and the three numbers to publish were
  re-measured and confirmed. It confirmed two medium issues, both now fixed:

  - Parity's commit bound could count a commit made after a failed gate as a
    pass. This matched a real stored C1 helper: it got 3 passes where 2 is
    right. The fix subtracts shown failures from the hidden passes, and ignores
    commits made by functions that never run the gate.
  - score-run's dp12, a pre-existing bug: it credited the first implement commit
    with gate runs from earlier stages. It now counts implement-stage runs
    only, the same window parity uses.

  Round-8 lows also fixed:
  - Each commit now belongs to one call in parity, as in score-run.
  - The publisher refuses to publish an old parity.json (one with no recorded
    evaluator) under 1.3 or later.
  - Capture forms like local out=$(…) are read, and so are quoted for-list
    items.
  - A real heredoc after a multi-line commit message is no longer missed.
  - A PowerShell here-string no longer runs on into a later one.
  - >&2 no longer counts as hiding a verdict, since stderr is in the tool
    output.
  - A slow scan on long PowerShell lists is fixed.
  - The telemetry README documents the evaluator rule.

  Verified:

  Check: Full suite
  Result: 211 passed, 75 skipped, 4 failed. The 4 need pwsh, which isn't
    installed here, and fail at HEAD too.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: Parity on the 7 C2/C3 runs
  Result: Identical to before: C2 bridge 97.03, C3 Opus 36.84, C3 GPT 76.67, all

    recording parity-eval/1.3.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: score-run over every stored baseline
  Result: The same 5 real runs go fail→pass.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: Mock runs
  Result: Six mock runs (claude-mock-5-5) now fail dp12. Each makes two commits
    after one gate run, so failing is correct; HEAD passed them only by
  borrowing
     an earlier stage's gate run. No doc or test expects them to pass.

  Commit split: five commits, with every intermediate tree tested:

  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────┐
  │              After commit               │ Tests passed │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ 1 fix subject                           │ 164          │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ 2 Run Summary                           │ 168          │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ 3 shared readers                        │ 206          │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ 4 implement-stage window (both scorers) │ 208          │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ 5 evaluator version                     │ 211          │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────┘

  Tree 5 is byte-identical to the working tree. A few reviewer lows I left as
  documented, deliberate choices rather than code changes:

  - Once any verdict is shown, hidden passes of literal gate calls are not
    credited.
  - A failure that is itself hidden can't be detected.

  Next steps:

  1. Commit the five, running a secret scan before each.
  2. Re-score the three runs with validation reruns.
  3. Dry-run, then publish parity-eval/1.3 with readback verification.
  4. Update the C2 README, which still says 94.24 and parity-eval/1.2.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  - Why the mock flips are a good sign. A fix that only ever moves results
    fail→pass is hard to tell apart from a lenient scorer. Six pass→fail flips
    on synthetic runs, each traced to an actual rule violation, show the window
    fix tightened the check as well as loosening it.
  - Bounding instead of guessing. Each round's medium findings came from
    inferring hidden behaviour. The fixes that held all bound a hidden quantity
    by something observed: hidden passes are at most the helper calls minus the
    shown failures, and at most the commits made. When the evidence is missing,
    they credit nothing.
